{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012wRuDN6jvVxbWKWX6uuVbS","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Net Health / RestorixHealth — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://www.nethealth.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Net Health Systems is a Pittsburgh-based healthcare software company founded in 1993 that specializes in EHR and analytics solutions for specialty care settings — primarily wound care, rehab therapy (PT/OT/SLP), employee health, and post-acute care. The company serves approximately 23,000 organizations and claims to work with 98% of the largest hospital chains. Net Health has approximately 630 employees and has raised over $1 billion in funding. In December 2017, Net Health was acquired by The Carlyle Group, Level Equity, and Silversmith Capital Partners from Spectrum Equity. The company has grown through numerous acquisitions, including Wound Care Strategies/TPS EMR (2012, which gave them dominant wound care market share), Tissue Analytics (2020, automated wound imaging), Casamba (2021, rehab therapy EMR), and PointRight (2021, skilled nursing analytics). Net Health is headquartered at 40 24th St., 1st Fl., Pittsburgh, PA 15222.\n     9→\n    10→RestorixHealth is a separate company — a wound care management services company (not a software company) founded in 1997 by physicians. They develop and manage outpatient wound care centers in partnership with hospitals and health systems, providing a turnkey outsourced model. RestorixHealth partners with 280+ hospitals across 39 states, including 100+ Critical Access Hospitals. In 2021, RestorixHealth merged with American Medical Technologies (AMT), creating a combined entity serving 6,250+ facilities across all 50 states. RestorixHealth is headquartered in White Plains, NY and has had multiple private equity owners (Sverica Capital, Leonard Green & Partners, Cressey & Company, One Equity Partners).\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Net Health WoundExpert\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 9836 (version 7.0, certified 2018-12-31)\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→WoundExpert (also marketed as \"Net Health Wound Care\") is a **specialty wound care EHR** — not a general-purpose EHR. It is a cloud/web-based electronic health record system purpose-built for wound care providers. Net Health claims it is \"the most trusted wound care EHR\" and \"trusted by more wound care professionals than any other.\" It is designed to supplement or interface with a facility's primary hospital EHR (Epic, Cerner, etc.) rather than replace it. The product is an ASP.NET web application.\n    19→\n    20→WoundExpert is ONC-certified for a broad set of criteria including clinical data (CPOE, demographics, drug interaction checking, family health history, implantable devices), transitions of care (C-CDA, clinical information reconciliation, e-prescribing), patient portal/API access (FHIR R4 via Darena Health partnership), clinical quality measures, and public health reporting. This is a comprehensive certification covering 37 criteria.\n    21→\n    22→### Users & Market\n    23→\n    24→**Primary users:** Hospital outpatient wound care centers, wound care management companies (like RestorixHealth), long-term care facilities, home health agencies, post-acute care settings, and private wound care practices.\n    25→\n    26→**Market position:** WoundExpert is the dominant specialty EHR for wound care in the US, with claims of ~90% market penetration in wound care. One source cites over 20,000 physicians across more than 1,000 facilities; another says over 2,000 facilities supporting over $2 billion in health services annually. The 2012 acquisition of Wound Care Strategies combined two major wound care software companies, giving Net Health >50% of US wound clinics at that time. Net Health has been collecting wound care data for over 20 years, creating what they describe as \"one of the largest condition-specific databases in the world.\"\n    27→\n    28→**Day-to-day users:** Wound care physicians, nurses, clinical staff at wound care centers, billing/coding staff, and practice managers.\n    29→\n    30→**Third-party ratings:** Capterra rates it 4.4/5 based on 15 reviews. It is described as \"the gold standard in managing wound care.\" Reviews note occasional documentation-save issues, performance concerns with large data volumes, high cost for smaller practices, and some usability challenges for new users.\n    31→\n    32→### Modules & Functionality\n    33→\n    34→**Clinical Documentation** (per vendor materials and FindEMR listing):\n    35→- Wound assessment tools: size, depth, tissue type, wound location, etiology, healing stage progression\n    36→- Treatment plan creation and management\n    37→- Wound dressing recommendations\n    38→- Progress notes with customizable templates and automatic coding\n    39→- Evidence-based clinical practice guidelines built in\n    40→- Medication management\n    41→\n    42→**Wound Imaging** (via Tissue Analytics acquisition, 2020):\n    43→- Mobile photo capture of wounds\n    44→- Automated wound measurement (reducing subjective manual measurement)\n    45→- Visual wound progression tracking over time\n    46→- Predictive analytics\n    47→\n    48→**Scheduling** (per FindEMR):\n    49→- Daily, weekly, monthly schedule views\n    50→- Appointment creation, management, and rescheduling\n    51→- expense tracking\n    57→- Compliance and regulatory support for billing\n    58→- Note: RestorixHealth's case study emphasizes their revenue cycle management integration with the platform, including certified coders and denial management specialists\n    59→\n    60→**Patient Engagement:**\n    61→- Patient portal (at patientportal.nethealth.com) — certified under (e)(1)\n    62→- Patient education materials\n    63→- Third-party app connectivity via FHIR API\n    64→\n    65→**Analytics and Reporting:**\n    66→- Customizable reports\n    67→- Benchmarking across facilities\n    68→- Performance insights\n    69→\n    70→**Interoperability** (per vendor interoperability page):\n    71→- Eight primary interface types: ADT (Admission/Discharge/Transfer), Billing, C-CDA, Clinical Documentation, Ordered Results, Scheduling, Transcription, Unsolicited Results\n    72→- Pre-built integrations with: Epic, Cerner, Allscripts, Athenahealth, MedBridge, Netsmart, PointClickCare\n    73→- FHIR R4 API (certified, via Darena Health partnership) supporting USCDI v3 data classes\n    74→- HL7 interfaces for hospital information system integration\n    75→\n    76→### Data & Content\n certified (a)(5)\n    81→- **Wound assessments** — wound size, depth, tissue type, location, etiology, healing stage; this is the core clinical data\n    82→- **Wound photographs and measurements** — via Tissue Analytics integration; automated wound imaging with progression tracking\n    83→- **Treatment plans** — wound dressings, medications, interventions, care plans\n    84→- **Progress notes and clinical documentation** — with customizable templates\n    85→- **Medication data** — certified for CPOE (a)(1) and drug interaction checking (a)(4)\n    86→- **Lab and diagnostic imaging orders/results** — certified for CPOE lab (a)(2) and CPOE diagnostic imaging (a)(3)\n    87→- **Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists** — certified (a)(5)\n    88→- **Family health history** — certified (a)(12)\n    89→- **Implantable device list** — certified (a)(14)\n    90→- **Scheduling/appointment data** — appointment creation, reminders, rescheduling\n    91→- **Billing and financial data** — charges, claims, payments, revenue tracking\n    92→- **Transitions of care documents** — C-CDA documents, certified (b)(1), (b)(2), (b)(3)\n    93→- **Clinical quality measure data** — certified (c)(1), (c)(2), (c)(3)\n    94→- **Public health reporting data** — certified (h)(1)\n    95→- **USCDI data elements** — via FHIR R4 API (g)(10): includes allergies, medications, conditions, procedures, vital signs, etc.\n    96→- **Audit logs** — certified for (d) criteria including authentication, access logging, encryption\n    97→- **Hyperbaric oxygen therapy records** — mentioned in context of RestorixHealth's services and CutisCare acquisition\n    98→\n    99→The vendor's interoperability page describes eight interface types, which imply the system stores or exchanges: ADT data, billing data, C-CDA documents, clinical documentation, ordered results, scheduling data, transcription data, and unsolicited results. The patient portal implies patient-accessible health records are maintained.\n   100→\n   101→---\n   102→\n   103→## Product: WoundDocs (RestorixHealth)\n   104→\n   105→CHPL ID: 10234 (version 7.0, certified 2019-12-24)\n   106→\n   107→### What It Is\n   108→\n   109→WoundDocs is **a white-labeled/OEM instance of Net Health's WoundExpert**, branded and deployed specifically for RestorixHealth's network of wound care centers. The evidence for this is conclusive:\n   110→\n   111→1. **URL path proof:** The WoundDocs login at `wounddocs.restorixhealth.com` redirects internally to paths containing `/WoundExpert/` (e.g., `/WoundExpert/Misc/OneUiRedirect.aspx` and `ReturnUrl=/WoundExpert/`).\n   112→2. **Net Health branding:** The WoundDocs login page displays the Net Health logo and Net Health browser compatibility notices.\n   113→3. **Net Health case study:** Net Health explicitly states RestorixHealth uses \"Net Health Wound Care\" as their EHR platform.\n   114→4. **Identical certification:** WoundDocs has exactly the same 37 certified criteria and version number (7.0) as WoundExpert, certified one year later under RestorixHealth's own developer code (2272 vs. Net Health's 2815).\n   115→\n   116→RestorixHealth markets it as \"WoundDocs Specialty EMR\" and describes it as \"an advanced, wound-specific EMR, supported by evidence-based clinical practice guidelines, that interfaces with your current EMR and provides physicians with the information they need for informed medical decision-making.\"\n   117→\n   118→### Users & Market\n   119→\n   120→WoundDocs is used across the **280+ hospitals and healthcare facilities** that partner with RestorixHealth for wound center management across 39 states. Hospitals do not typically purchase WoundDocs independently — it comes bundled with RestorixHealth's comprehensive wound center management package, which includes clinical staffing, operational support, revenue cycle management, and marketing.\n   121→\n   122→Specific user populations include:\n   123→- Hospital-based outpatient wound care center clinicians managed by RestorixHealth\n   124→- Critical Access Hospital wound care staff (100+ facilities in 21 states)\n   125→- Wound care physicians and clinical staff at partner facilities\n   126→- Revenue cycle and billing staff\n   127→\n   128→### Modules & Functionality\n   129→\n   130→Functionally identical to WoundExpert (see above), since it is the same underlying software. RestorixHealth's website additionally emphasizes:\n   131→\n   132→- **Revenue cycle management integration:** RestorixHealth has \"one of the largest teams of Revenue Cycle Directors, denial management and reimbursement specialists and certified coders and auditors in the industry,\" all working within the WoundDocs/WoundExpert platform.\n   133→- **Standardized workflows across facilities:** The platform is configured to support RestorixHealth's standardized wound care protocols across their entire network.\n   134→- **Hospital EHR interfacing:** Emphasis on integration with existing hospital information systems at each partner site.\n   135→\n   136→### Data & Content\n   137→\n   138→Identical to WoundExpert (see above) — it is the same software platform. Any data WoundExpert can store, WoundDocs can store. The RestorixHealth deployment may additionally emphasize:\n   139→- Hyperbaric oxygen therapy records (RestorixHealth acquired CutisCare, a hyperbaric specialist)\n   140→- Revenue cycle and billing data specific to wound center management\n   141→- Cross-facility benchmarking data across RestorixHealth's 280+ hospital network\n   142→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The company serves approximately 23,000 organizations and claims to work with 98% of the largest hospital chains. Net Health has approximately 630 employees and has raised over $1 billion in funding. In December 2017, Net Health was acquired by The Carlyle Group, Level Equity, and Silversmith Capital Partners from Spectrum Equity. The company has grown through numerous acquisitions, including Wound Care Strategies/TPS EMR (2012, which gave them dominant wound care market share), Tissue Analytics (2020, automated wound imaging), Casamba (2021, rehab therapy EMR), and PointRight (2021, skilled nursing analytics). Net Health is headquartered at 40 24th St., 1st Fl., Pittsburgh, PA 15222.\n\nRestorixHealth is a separate company — a wound care management services company (not a software company) founded in 1997 by physicians. They develop and manage outpatient wound care centers in partnership with hospitals and health systems, providing a turnkey outsourced model. RestorixHealth partners with 280+ hospitals across 39 states, including 100+ Critical Access Hospitals. In 2021, RestorixHealth merged with American Medical Technologies (AMT), creating a combined entity serving 6,250+ facilities across all 50 states. RestorixHealth is headquartered in White Plains, NY and has had multiple private equity owners (Sverica Capital, Leonard Green & Partners, Cressey & Company, One Equity Partners).\n\n## Product: Net Health WoundExpert\n\nCHPL ID: 9836 (version 7.0, certified 2018-12-31)\n\n### What It Is\n\nWoundExpert (also marketed as \"Net Health Wound Care\") is a **specialty wound care EHR** — not a general-purpose EHR. It is a cloud/web-based electronic health record system purpose-built for wound care providers. Net Health claims it is \"the most trusted wound care EHR\" and \"trusted by more wound care professionals than any other.\" It is designed to supplement or interface with a facility's primary hospital EHR (Epic, Cerner, etc.) rather than replace it. The product is an ASP.NET web application.\n\nWoundExpert is ONC-certified for a broad set of criteria including clinical data (CPOE, demographics, drug interaction checking, family health history, implantable devices), transitions of care (C-CDA, clinical information reconciliation, e-prescribing), patient portal/API access (FHIR R4 via Darena Health partnership), clinical quality measures, and public health reporting. This is a comprehensive certification covering 37 criteria.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Primary users:** Hospital outpatient wound care centers, wound care management companies (like RestorixHealth), long-term care facilities, home health agencies, post-acute care settings, and private wound care practices.\n\n**Market position:** WoundExpert is the dominant specialty EHR for wound care in the US, with claims of ~90% market penetration in wound care. One source cites over 20,000 physicians across more than 1,000 facilities; another says over 2,000 facilities supporting over $2 billion in health services annually. The 2012 acquisition of Wound Care Strategies combined two major wound care software companies, giving Net Health >50% of US wound clinics at that time. Net Health has been collecting wound care data for over 20 years, creating what they describe as \"one of the largest condition-specific databases in the world.\"\n\n**Day-to-day users:** Wound care physicians, nurses, clinical staff at wound care centers, billing/coding staff, and practice managers.\n\n**Third-party ratings:** Capterra rates it 4.4/5 based on 15 reviews. It is described as \"the gold standard in managing wound care.\" Reviews note occasional documentation-save issues, performance concerns with large data volumes, high cost for smaller practices, and some usability challenges for new users.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\n**Clinical Documentation** (per vendor materials and FindEMR listing):\n- Wound assessment tools: size, depth, tissue type, wound location, etiology, healing stage progression\n- Treatment plan creation and management\n- Wound dressing recommendations\n- Progress notes with customizable templates and automatic coding\n- Evidence-based clinical practice guidelines built in\n- Medication management\n\n**Wound Imaging** (via Tissue Analytics acquisition, 2020):\n- Mobile photo capture of wounds\n- Automated wound measurement (reducing subjective manual measurement)\n- Visual wound progression tracking over time\n- Predictive analytics\n\n**Scheduling** (per FindEMR):\n- Daily, weekly, monthly schedule views\n- Appointment creation, management, and rescheduling\n- Automated appointment reminders\n\n**Financial/Billing** (per FindEMR):\n- Invoice generation and payment processing\n- Insurance claims management\n- Revenue and expense tracking\n- Compliance and regulatory support for billing\n- Note: RestorixHealth's case study emphasizes their revenue cycle management integration with the platform, including certified coders and denial management specialists\n\n**Patient Engagement:**\n- Patient portal (at patientportal.nethealth.com) — certified under (e)(1)\n- Patient education materials\n- Third-party app connectivity via FHIR API\n\n**Analytics and Reporting:**\n- Customizable reports\n- Benchmarking across facilities\n- Performance insights\n\n**Interoperability** (per vendor interoperability page):\n- Eight primary interface types: ADT (Admission/Discharge/Transfer), Billing, C-CDA, Clinical Documentation, Ordered Results, Scheduling, Transcription, Unsolicited Results\n- Pre-built integrations with: Epic, Cerner, Allscripts, Athenahealth, MedBridge, Netsmart, PointClickCare\n- FHIR R4 API (certified, via Darena Health partnership) supporting USCDI v3 data classes\n- HL7 interfaces for hospital information system integration\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on certified criteria, vendor materials, and product feature descriptions, WoundExpert stores:\n\n- **Patient demographics** — via ADT interfaces and direct entry; certified (a)(5)\n- **Wound assessments** — wound size, depth, tissue type, location, etiology, healing stage; this is the core clinical data\n- **Wound photographs and measurements** — via Tissue Analytics integration; automated wound imaging with progression tracking\n- **Treatment plans** — wound dressings, medications, interventions, care plans\n- **Progress notes and clinical documentation** — with customizable templates\n- **Medication data** — certified for CPOE (a)(1) and drug interaction checking (a)(4)\n- **Lab and diagnostic imaging orders/results** — certified for CPOE lab (a)(2) and CPOE diagnostic imaging (a)(3)\n- **Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists** — certified (a)(5)\n- **Family health history** — certified (a)(12)\n- **Implantable device list** — certified (a)(14)\n- **Scheduling/appointment data** — appointment creation, reminders, rescheduling\n- **Billing and financial data** — charges, claims, payments, revenue tracking\n- **Transitions of care documents** — C-CDA documents, certified (b)(1), (b)(2), (b)(3)\n- **Clinical quality measure data** — certified (c)(1), (c)(2), (c)(3)\n- **Public health reporting data** — certified (h)(1)\n- **USCDI data elements** — via FHIR R4 API (g)(10): includes allergies, medications, conditions, procedures, vital signs, etc.\n- **Audit logs** — certified for (d) criteria including authentication, access logging, encryption\n- **Hyperbaric oxygen therapy records** — mentioned in context of RestorixHealth's services and CutisCare acquisition\n\nThe vendor's interoperability page describes eight interface types, which imply the system stores or exchanges: ADT data, billing data, C-CDA documents, clinical documentation, ordered results, scheduling data, transcription data, and unsolicited results. The patient portal implies patient-accessible health records are maintained.\n\n---\n\n## Product: WoundDocs (RestorixHealth)\n\nCHPL ID: 10234 (version 7.0, certified 2019-12-24)\n\n### What It Is\n\nWoundDocs is **a white-labeled/OEM instance of Net Health's WoundExpert**, branded and deployed specifically for RestorixHealth's network of wound care centers. The evidence for this is conclusive:\n\n1. **URL path proof:** The WoundDocs login at `wounddocs.restorixhealth.com` redirects internally to paths containing `/WoundExpert/` (e.g., `/WoundExpert/Misc/OneUiRedirect.aspx` and `ReturnUrl=/WoundExpert/`).\n2. **Net Health branding:** The WoundDocs login page displays the Net Health logo and Net Health browser compatibility notices.\n3. **Net Health case study:** Net Health explicitly states RestorixHealth uses \"Net Health Wound Care\" as their EHR platform.\n4. **Identical certification:** WoundDocs has exactly the same 37 certified criteria and version number (7.0) as WoundExpert, certified one year later under RestorixHealth's own developer code (2272 vs. Net Health's 2815).\n\nRestorixHealth markets it as \"WoundDocs Specialty EMR\" and describes it as \"an advanced, wound-specific EMR, supported by evidence-based clinical practice guidelines, that interfaces with your current EMR and provides physicians with the information they need for informed medical decision-making.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\nWoundDocs is used across the **280+ hospitals and healthcare facilities** that partner with RestorixHealth for wound center management across 39 states. Hospitals do not typically purchase WoundDocs independently — it comes bundled with RestorixHealth's comprehensive wound center management package, which includes clinical staffing, operational support, revenue cycle management, and marketing.\n\nSpecific user populations include:\n- Hospital-based outpatient wound care center clinicians managed by RestorixHealth\n- Critical Access Hospital wound care staff (100+ facilities in 21 states)\n- Wound care physicians and clinical staff at partner facilities\n- Revenue cycle and billing staff\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nFunctionally identical to WoundExpert (see above), since it is the same underlying software. RestorixHealth's website additionally emphasizes:\n\n- **Revenue cycle management integration:** RestorixHealth has \"one of the largest teams of Revenue Cycle Directors, denial management and reimbursement specialists and certified coders and auditors in the industry,\" all working within the WoundDocs/WoundExpert platform.\n- **Standardized workflows across facilities:** The platform is configured to support RestorixHealth's standardized wound care protocols across their entire network.\n- **Hospital EHR interfacing:** Emphasis on integration with existing hospital information systems at each partner site.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nIdentical to WoundExpert (see above) — it is the same software platform. \n WoundExpert version 7.0  was certified on December 31, 2018, by Drummond Group and updated on December 29, 2022 to Cures Edition. Certificate Number:  15.04.04.2815.Woun.07.00.1.181231  Modules Tested:  170.315 (a)(1-5, 12, 14); (b)(1-3, 10-11); (c)(1-3); (d)(1-9, 11-13); (e)(1, 3); (g)(2-6 (h)(1)  Clinical Quality Measures tested:  22v13, 50v12, 68v7, 122v6, 138v6, 139v6, 156v6, 165v6  Additional software used:  NLM API, Secure Exchange Solutions SES Direct, First Databank, SureScripts e-Prescribing, Net Health Patient Portal, Surescripts ePrescribing, Identity Server, Swagger, Identity Server 4, Secure Exchange Solutions SES HISP, Darena Health  \n Electronic Health Information Export Single patient and patient population electronic health information can be exported in the form of an “export zip” file which&nbsp;contains&nbsp;files in a machine-readable format&nbsp;in accordance with §170.315(b)(10) – Electronic Health Information export of the ONC 2015 Edition Cures Update Certification Criteria.&nbsp; \n The content and format of the data contained within the export zip file depending on number of patients selected and software functionality in use. The EHI Export zipped folder contains a folder for each patient that contains csv files of EHI data  all image files from the patient record  all custom scans on file  EHIDataExtract_DataDictionary.xlsx  ExportSummary.txt \n Costs and Criteria Specific Considerations 170.315 (b)(3) &#8211;&nbsp;Base&nbsp;licensing of the&nbsp;WoundExpert&nbsp;EHR includes access to a third-party medications and allergies database via our partner, First Databank. Net Health contracts with First Databank&nbsp;in order to&nbsp;extend functionality such as e-prescribing and drug interactions to our&nbsp;WoundExpert&nbsp;clients.&nbsp;Alternative medication or allergy databases via the Wound Care application are not supported&nbsp;at this time.&nbsp; \n 170.315 (a)(14) &#8211;&nbsp;Net&nbsp;Health uses the NLM to provide its Eligible Clinicians (ECs) access to a list of implantable devices for documentation in&nbsp;WoundExpert.&nbsp; \n 170.315&nbsp;(h)(1) &#8211;&nbsp;ECs&nbsp;will&nbsp;be required&nbsp;to pay an ongoing annual, per clinician, flat fee to exchange secure messages and medical record files via a Direct Account with our partner Secure Exchange Solutions (SES). This functionality allows users to send and receive Direct-based messages to/from other users of certified health IT systems. Direct messages may include clinical data, notes, and transitions of care and&nbsp;dischargesummary documents. Net Health’s contract with SES also offers our customers the ability to utilize an alternative Health Information Service Provider (HISP).The&nbsp;Promoting Interoperability Grid Report is&nbsp;updated on&nbsp;every Tuesday, Thursday, Friday and Sunday night, due to the volume of data; requests for more frequent delivery can be accommodated. The Promoting Interoperability Grid Report provides the numerator, denominator, resulting percentage, and list of patients meeting and not meeting the measure for all automated measures.&nbsp;It is run per clinician&nbsp;and also&nbsp;includes a text description of how the data is captured&nbsp;per&nbsp;measure.&nbsp; \n 170.315 (e)(1) &#8211;&nbsp;Net&nbsp;Health allows patients to grant access&nbsp;for&nbsp;WoundExpert&nbsp;data to third-party application (app) developers. The Net Health Patient Portal is used to grant access to the data, per patient, per app, including the ability to specify the type of data and to revoke access.&nbsp;Net Health makes its FHIR based Application Programming Interface (API) publicly available through partnership with Darena Health, which&nbsp;leveragesFHIR&nbsp;Server, connected to&nbsp;WoundExpert&nbsp;Patient and Visit FHIR API, to enable&nbsp;app developer workspaces. Enablement includes a one-time&nbsp;implementation fee and ongoing monthly&nbsp;per&nbsp;provider fee to cover costs of the solution.&nbsp; \n WoundExpert&nbsp;relies upon Net Health’s Patient Portal Version 1 to meet the View, Download and Transmit Measure (170.315.e.1).&nbsp;&nbsp;Net Health’s Patient Portal is included with the Base Licensing of&nbsp;WoundExpert&nbsp;EHR.&nbsp; \n\t\t/* UMD export */\n!function o(n,c,a){function u(t,e){if(!c[t]){if(!n[t]){var r=\"function\"==typeof require&&require;if(!e&&r)return r(t,!0);if(s)return s(t,!0);throw(e=new Error(\"Cannot find module '\"+t+\"'\")).code=\"MODULE_NOT_FOUND\",e}r=c[t]={exports:{}},n[t][0].call(r.exports,function(e){return u(n[t][1][e]||e)},r,r.exports,o,n,c,a)}return c[t].exports}for(var s=\"function\"==typeof require&&require,e=0;e {var e=t.selector;document.querySelectorAll(e).forEach(e=>{e.setAttribute(\"data-rocket-lazy-bg-\"+t.hash,\"excluded\")})}),document.querySelector(\"#wpr-lazyload-bg-container\"));var o=rocket_lazyload_css_data.threshold||300;const u=new IntersectionObserver(e=>{e.forEach(t=>{t.isIntersecting&&c.filter(e=>t.target.matches(e.selector)).map(t=>{var e;t&&((e=document.createElement(\"style\")).textContent=t.style,a.insertAdjacentElement(\"afterend\",e),t.elements.forEach(e=>{u.unobserve(e),e.setAttribute(\"data-rocket-lazy-bg-\"+t.hash,\"loaded\")}))})})},{rootMargin:o+\"px\"});function n(){0 {try{document.querySelectorAll(t.selector).forEach(e=>{\"loaded\"!==e.getAttribute(\"data-rocket-lazy-bg-\"+t.hash)&&\"excluded\"!==e.getAttribute(\"data-rocket-lazy-bg-\"+t.hash)&&(u.observe(e),(t.elements||=[]).push(e))})}catch(e){console.error(e)}})}n(),function(){const r=window.MutationObserver;return function(e,t){if(e&&1===e.nodeType)return(t=new r(t)).observe(e,{attributes:!0,childList:!0,subtree:!0}),t}}()(document.querySelector(\"body\"),n)}},{}]},{},[1]);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false}}

data-kb-block=\\\"kb-adv-heading27833_691866-c0\\\"><strong>What has occurred?\\u00a0</strong>\\u00a0<br><a href=\\\"http://www.nethealth.com/\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" rel=\\\"noreferrer noopener\\\">Net Health</a>, a healthcare technology company that provides software solutions to enhance efficiency, compliance, and patient outcomes, headquartered in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, has acquired Limber.<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Why did Net Health acquire Limber?\\u00a0</strong><br>For more than 35 years, Net Health has been dedicated to delivering high-value software solutions that empower providers to efficiently manage operations and improve outcomes. Limber shares in this mission, with a focus on supporting musculoskeletal (MSK) and physical therapy through improved home exercise engagement and remote monitoring outside the clinic.<br><br>Both organizations serve the rehab therapy market with complementary strengths. Net Health delivers a comprehensive clinical, operational, and financial platform across the continuum of care, while Limber brings deep expertise in remote therapeutic monitoring (RTM) and patient\\u00a0experience. Together, we now support clinicians and patients across the full continuum\\u2014from Hospital to Home\\u2014enabling more effective hybrid care models and advancing value-based outcomes.<br><br>This partnership unites two leading outcomes platforms to deliver stronger results and unparalleled benchmarking for both enterprise health systems and private practices. It extends the value we provide to our clients\\u2014especially between visits\\u2014by filling gaps that traditional EHRs leave behind. We are excited to share best practices, enhance innovation, and continue doing what we do best: helping our clients grow their organizations.\\u00a0\\u00a0\\u00a0\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>How will this change affect Limber? Will I be required to change software?\\u00a0</strong><br>There are no planned changes to the Limber solutions you rely on today. You will continue to benefit from the products and tools you know and the expertise of the team you trust.\\u00a0As we integrate Limber into our company structure, you will also begin to see new benefits.\\u00a0We are committed to investing in future generations of our products, including an aligned roadmap to help our clients address their ever-changing market needs.<br><br>Additionally, all Limber clients will enjoy the benefits of Net Health\\u2019s investments in client experience and support infrastructure. Net Health has decades of experience helping organizations like yours succeed and is committed to ensuring the Limber team has the resources it needs to\\u00a0continue supporting your success.\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>What will happen to the Limber team? How will Limber fit into Net Health\\u2019s corporate structure?</strong>\\u00a0<br>The Limber team, under the continued leadership of Michael Gruner, President, and Nirav Modi, SVP of Operations, will operate largely independently as part of the Rehab Therapy portfolio. This structure allows the team to maintain the agility and focus that have contributed to their success, while gaining access to the broader resources, infrastructure, and support of Net Health.<br><br>Over time, we will align on shared priorities and explore opportunities for product collaboration, operational support, and innovation, always with the goal of enhancing client value. For now, it\\u2019s business as usual for the Limber team, with no immediate changes to how you work with them.<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Whom do I call for Limber support?</strong>\\u00a0<br>You can continue to call the same contacts that you always have. They will continue to support the platform and will be working closely with the Net Health team to ensure all your questions are answered and your needs are met.\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Whom should I contact with questions that haven\\u2019t been addressed or for more information?</strong>\\u00a0<br>If you have additional questions related to the acquisition,\\u00a0please reach out to Nirav Modi, or send an email to\\u00a0<a href=\\\"mailto:questions@nethealth.com\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" rel=\\\"noreferrer noopener\\\">questions@nethealth.com</a>.\\u00a0</p>\\n\",\n has-alpha-channel-opacity has-theme-palette-8-background-color has-background is-style-wide\\\"/>\\n\\n\\n<style>.wp-block-kadence-advancedheading.kt-adv-heading27833_691866-c0, data-kb-block=\\\"kb-adv-heading27833_691866-c0\\\"><strong>What has occurred?\\u00a0</strong>\\u00a0<br><a href=\\\"http://www.nethealth.com/\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" rel=\\\"noreferrer noopener\\\">Net Health</a>, a healthcare technology company that provides software solutions to enhance efficiency, compliance, and patient outcomes, headquartered in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, has acquired Limber.<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Why did Net Health acquire Limber?\\u00a0</strong><br>For more than 35 years, Net Health has been dedicated to delivering high-value software solutions that empower providers to efficiently manage operations and improve outcomes. Limber shares in this mission, with a focus on supporting musculoskeletal (MSK) and physical therapy through improved home exercise engagement and remote monitoring outside the clinic.<br><br>Both organizations serve the rehab therapy market with complementary strengths. Net Health delivers a comprehensive clinical, operational, and financial platform across the continuum of care, while Limber brings deep expertise in remote therapeutic monitoring (RTM) and patient\\u00a0experience. Together, we now support clinicians and patients across the full continuum\\u2014from Hospital to Home\\u2014enabling more effective hybrid care models and advancing value-based outcomes.<br><br>This partnership unites two leading outcomes platforms to deliver stronger results and unparalleled benchmarking for both enterprise health systems and private practices. It extends the value we provide to our clients\\u2014especially between visits\\u2014by filling gaps that traditional EHRs leave behind. We are excited to share best practices, enhance innovation, and continue doing what we do best: helping our clients grow their organizations.\\u00a0\\u00a0\\u00a0\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>How will this change affect Limber? Will I be required to change software?\\u00a0</strong><br>There are no planned changes to the Limber solutions you rely on today. You will continue to benefit from the products and tools you know and the expertise of the team you trust.\\u00a0As we integrate Limber into our company structure, you will also begin to see new benefits.\\u00a0We are committed to investing in future generations of our products, including an aligned roadmap to help our clients address their ever-changing market needs.<br><br>Additionally, all Limber clients will enjoy the benefits of Net Health\\u2019s investments in client experience and support infrastructure. Net Health has decades of experience helping organizations like yours succeed and is committed to ensuring the Limber team has the resources it needs to\\u00a0continue supporting your success.\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>What will happen to the Limber team? How will Limber fit into Net Health\\u2019s corporate structure?</strong>\\u00a0<br>The Limber team, under the continued leadership of Michael Gruner, President, and Nirav Modi, SVP of Operations, will operate largely independently as part of the Rehab Therapy portfolio. This structure allows the team to maintain the agility and focus that have contributed to their success, while gaining access to the broader resources, infrastructure, and support of Net Health.<br><br>Over time, we will align on shared priorities and explore opportunities for product collaboration, operational support, and innovation, always with the goal of enhancing client value. For now, it\\u2019s business as usual for the Limber team, with no immediate changes to how you work with them.<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Whom do I call for Limber support?</strong>\\u00a0<br>You can continue to call the same contacts that you always have. They will continue to support the platform and will be working closely with the Net Health team to ensure all your questions are answered and your needs are met.\\u00a0<br><br>\\u00a0<br><strong>Whom should I contact with questions that haven\\u2019t been addressed or for more information?</strong>\\u00a0<br>If you have additional questions related to the acquisition,\\u00a0please reach out to Nirav Modi, or send an email to\\u00a0<a href=\\\"mailto:questions@nethealth.com\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" rel=\\\"noreferrer noopener\\\">questions@nethealth.com</a>.\\u00a0</p>\\n\",\n            \"protected\": false\n        },\n        \"excerpt\": {\n            \"rendered\": \"<p>May 28, 2025&nbsp; The data dictionary is only included inside the export ZIP, not publicly downloadable.\n\n7. **Searched WordPress REST API** for additional EHI pages or media:\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://www.nethealth.com/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?search=ehi&per_page=10\"\n   curl -sL \"https://www.nethealth.com/wp-json/wp/v2/media?search=EHI&per_page=10\"\n   ```\n   Result: No EHI-related pages or media files found.\n\n8. **Tried common documentation paths** (`/ehi/`, `/ehi-export/`, `/data-dictionary/`, `/b10/`, etc.) — all returned 404.\n\n9. **Took full-page screenshot** of the certification page and saved page HTML and clean text extraction.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nThe EHI export documentation for Net Health WoundExpert consists entirely of a brief inline description on the ONC certification page. There are no downloadable artifacts — no PDF, no data dictionary, no schema, no sample exports, no user guide.\n\n### The EHI Export Description\n\nThe page states:\n\n> Single patient and patient population electronic health information can be exported in the form of an \"export zip\" file which contains files in a machine-readable format in accordance with §170.315(b)(10) – Electronic Health Information export of the ONC 2015 Edition Cures Update Certification Criteria.\n\n> The content and format of the data contained within the export zip file depending on number of patients selected and software functionality in use.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export is described as a ZIP file containing one folder per patient, with:\n\n1. **CSV files** of EHI data\n2. **Image files** from the patient record\n3. **Custom scans** on file\n4. **EHIDataExtract_DataDictionary.xlsx** — an Excel data dictionary\n5. **ExportSummary.txt** — an export summary\n\nThis is a proper (b)(10) approach — CSV-based bulk export of all EHI data, not a FHIR API repackaging. The format is straightforward and appropriate for a specialty wound care system. However, the public documentation tells us almost nothing about what data is actually in those CSVs.\n\n### What's NOT Documented Publicly\n\n- **Which CSV files are included** — no list of table/file names\n- **What columns each CSV contains** — no field definitions\n- **What data types are used** — no type information\n- **What coded values mean** — no value set documentation\n- **How relationships between CSVs are expressed** — no foreign key documentation\n- **What the data dictionary XLSX actually contains** — only that it exists\n- **How to perform the export** — no user instructions or screenshots\n- **Whether the export is complete** — no statement on which data domains are included\n\nThe data dictionary (EHIDataExtract_DataDictionary.xlsx) is bundled inside the export itself — meaning you can only see the documentation after you've already obtained an export. It is not publicly downloadable.\n\n### FHIR API (Separate from EHI Export)\n\nNet Health also has a separate FHIR R4 API certified under (g)(10), provided through partnership with Darena Health. This API supports USCDI v3 data classes and is documented at `https://docs.darena.health/`. This is a completely separate mechanism from the (b)(10) EHI export and covers only the standard USCDI subset of clinical data. The certification page correctly distinguishes between the two.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, WoundExpert stores the following clinical and billing data domains:\n\n| Domain | Covered by Export? | Evidence |\n|--------|-------------------|----------|\n| Patient demographics | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Wound assessments (size, depth, tissue type, location, etiology) | Unknown | Not documented — this is the CORE data for a wound care EHR |\n| Wound photographs and measurements | Likely yes | \"all image files from the patient record\" |\n| Treatment plans | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Progress notes / clinical documentation | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Medication data | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Lab/diagnostic imaging orders & results | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Family health history | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Implantable device list | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Billing and financial data | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Scheduling/appointment data | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Transitions of care documents (C-CDAs) | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Hyperbaric oxygen therapy records | Unknown | Not documented |\n| Custom scans | Likely yes | \"all custom scans on file\" |\n| Clinical quality measure data | Unknown | Not documented |\n\nThe documentation says \"csv files of EHI data\" without specifying which data. The phrase \"content and format ... depending on ... software functionality in use\" suggests the CSV contents may vary by which modules a facility uses, but no specifics are given. This makes independent coverage assessment impossible from the public documentation alone.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format:** ZIP containing CSV files + images + XLSX data dictionary + TXT summary\n- **Standard:** Ad-hoc vendor format (CSV), which is appropriate for (b)(10) — no requirement for FHIR or any standard\n- **Strengths:**\n  - CSV is universally readable\n  - Including a data dictionary XLSX in each export is thoughtful — it provides self-documenting exports\n  - Including all images and custom scans shows awareness of non-structured data\n  - Supporting both single-patient and population exports is correct per the regulation\n- **Weaknesses:**\n  - No public documentation of the CSV structure means no one can prepare to receive or process these exports in advance\n  - Relationships between CSV files are undocumented\n  - No sample export data available\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Very poor.** The public EHI export documentation is approximately 100 words — two short paragraphs on a certification page. This is among the most minimal (b)(10) documentation possible while still technically acknowledging the requirement exists.\n\nSpecific gaps:\n- No data dictionary (the one in the export is not publicly accessible)\n- No field definitions, data types, or cardinality\n- No value set documentation\n- No sample export files\n- No export instructions or user guide\n- No screenshots of the export interface\n- No API specification (the export is not API-based)\n- No worked examples\n\nA developer could not implement an import of this data based on the public documentation. They would need to obtain an actual export and reverse-engineer the CSV structure from the bundled data dictionary.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe documentation mentions that the export exists and describes what container types are in it (CSVs, images, scans, a data dictionary, a summary). That's it. There is zero granularity beyond this — no table names, no field names, no data types, no relationships, no value sets.\n\nThe one positive structural element is the mention of `EHIDataExtract_DataDictionary.xlsx` — this suggests Net Health has actually built a data dictionary, just not published it publicly. The export may be quite well-documented for those who have access to it.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nNet Health has taken the correct architectural approach to (b)(10) compliance: a CSV-based bulk export of all EHI data with an embedded data dictionary, rather than trying to shoehorn everything through their FHIR API (which they correctly treat as a separate (g)(10) mechanism). The inclusion of images, custom scans, and a per-export data dictionary shows genuine thought about what a complete export should contain.\n\nHowever, the public-facing documentation is functionally nonexistent. The certification page provides just enough information to confirm that the capability exists and that it uses CSVs, but nowhere near enough for a recipient to prepare for, validate, or process an export. The data dictionary — arguably the most important artifact — is locked inside the export itself and not publicly available.\n\nThis is a common pattern for specialty EHR vendors: the actual (b)(10) implementation may be solid, but the public documentation consists of a single paragraph on a certification page. For WoundExpert specifically, this is a missed opportunity — wound care data has specialized structures (wound measurements, tissue types, healing trajectories) that would benefit from clear documentation to enable data portability between wound care systems.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.nethealth.com/drummond-certified-for-meaningful-use-woundexpert/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (page is static HTML served by WordPress)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI export section is inline on the page, no clicks needed)\n- Anti-bot issues: Cloudflare present but no blocking observed with standard User-Agent\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- No downloadable files (PDF, XLSX, CSV, ZIP) linked from the certification page\n- Data dictionary (EHIDataExtract_DataDictionary.xlsx) exists only inside export ZIPs, not publicly hosted\n- WordPress REST API search for \"EHI\" returned no additional pages or media\n- Common documentation paths (/ehi/, /ehi-export/, /data-dictionary/, etc.) all 404\n- The FHIR API documentation (via Darena Health) is for (g)(10), not (b)(10)\n- Mandatory disclosures URL redirects to the same certification page\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":329,"cache_read_input_tokens":72722,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":329},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ab3a81b7-bf60-4419-9f47-1f19eed88fb4","uuid":"2c54e2ff-8b50-4b65-a625-5de5923893ae"}
